The Technical IntegrationYour repo has the voice assets. Here's the actual workflow:Python   Copy # ElevenLabs v3: Mode-switching via inline tags
import elevenlabs

def generate_response(character_id, mode, text):
    voice_id = CHARACTER_VOICES[character_id]  # Your cloned voice
    
    # Mode-to-tag mapping
    mode_tags = {
        "lullaby": "[whispers] [slowly]",
        "stem": "[excited]",
        "story_time": "[narrator voice]",
        "calm_breathe": "[softly] [slowly]",
        # ... 13 modes
    }
    
    tagged_text = f"{mode_tags[mode]} {text}"
    
    audio = elevenlabs.generate(
        voice=voice_id,
        text=tagged_text,
        model="eleven_v3",  # Expressive model
        voice_settings={
            "stability": 0.3,  # Low = more expressive
            "similarity_boost": 0.75
        }
    )
    return audio Python   Copy # Cartesia Sonic 3: Mode-switching via SSML
import cartesia

def generate_response(character_id, mode, text):
    voice_id = CHARACTER_VOICES[character_id]
    
    # SSML prosody mapping
    mode_ssml = {
        "lullaby": '<prosody rate="slow" pitch="low" volume="soft">',
        "stem": '<prosody rate="fast" pitch="high" volume="loud">',
        # ... 13 modes
    }
    
    ssml_text = f"{mode_ssml[mode]}{text}</prosody>"
    
    audio = cartesia.tts.generate(
        voice=voice_id,
        text=ssml_text,
        model="sonic-3",
        output_format="mp3_44100_128"
    )
    return audio Bottom LineTable  Copy   QuestionAnswer Best for your existing assets?ElevenLabs Professional Voice Cloning. **Assistant:** Got it — you want a PowerShell prompt for Kimi Code that sets up the voice agent backend with Groq + Cartesia, based on everything we just researched. Let me write this as a clean, copy-pasteable script you can feed directly into Kimi Code.Kimi Code PowerShell Prompt: Voice Agent Backendpowershell   Copy <#
.SYNOPSIS
    Casa Companion Voice Agent Backend Setup
    Stack: Groq (LLM) + Cartesia (TTS) + Deepgram (STT) + Redis (session state)
    No Azure. No ElevenLabs for production. ESP32-S3 compatible.

.DESCRIPTION
    This prompt initializes the voice agent backend for Casa Companion.
    Handles: STT streaming, LLM persona routing, TTS with emotion control,
    session state across devices, NFC medallion mode switching.
    
    Designed for: Kimi Code PowerShell integration
    Target: Fly.io / Hetzner / any VPS
    Language: Python (FastAPI), Redis, WebSockets

.NOTES
    Created: 2026-06-13
    Stack version: v1.0-cloud-agnostic
#>

# =============================================================================
# KIMI CODE PROMPT - PASTE EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE INTO KIMI CODE
# =============================================================================

Write-Host @"
=== CASA COMPANION VOICE AGENT BACKEND ===
Stack: Groq + Cartesia + Deepgram + Redis
No Azure. No ElevenLabs production. ESP32-S3 ready.

GENERATE THE FOLLOWING:
"@ -ForegroundColor Cyan

# --- 1. PROJECT STRUCTURE ---
Write-Host "`n[1] PROJECT STRUCTURE" -ForegroundColor Yellow
@"
Create directory structure:
/voice-agent/
├── app/
│   ├── __init__.py
│   ├── main.py              # FastAPI entry
│   ├── config.py            # Env vars, API keys
│   ├── models/
│   │   ├── session.py       # Redis session schema
│   │   ├── character.py     # 10 characters + 13 modes
│   │   └── device.py        # ESP32, Tap Board, TV
│   ├── services/
│   │   ├── stt.py           # Deepgram streaming
│   │   ├── llm.py           # Groq prompt router
│   │   ├── tts.py           # Cartesia SSML prosody
│   │   ├── session.py       # Redis state manager
│   │   └── nfc.py         # Medallion decoder
│   ├── routers/
│   │   ├── websocket.py     # ESP32 audio stream
│   │   ├── http.py          # REST fallback
│   │   └── health.py        # Status checks
│   └── utils/
│       ├── audio.py         # I2S encoding, chunking
│       └── copa.py          # COPPA compliance (ephemeral)
├── tests/
├── docker/

│   └── docker-compose.yml   # Redis + app
├── scripts/
│   └── deploy.sh            # Fly.io deploy
├── requirements.txt
└── .env.example

"@

# --- 2. CORE CONFIGURATION ---
Write-Host "`n[2] CORE CONFIGURATION (config.py)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
@"
Environment variables:
- GROQ_API_KEY              # From groq.com
- CARTESIA_API_KEY          # From cartesia.ai
- DEEPGRAM_API_KEY          # From deepgram.com
- REDIS_URL                 # redis://localhost:6379 or Fly.io Redis
- SESSION_TTL_SECONDS=3600  # 1 hour session expiry
- MAX_AUDIO_CHUNK_MS=100    # ESP32 I2S DMA buffer
- TTS_MAX_CHARS=500         # Cartesia limit
- WAKE_WORDS=orsetto,gufo,corvo,drago,leone,coniglio,tartaruga,elefante,delfino,xolo

COPPA compliance flags:
- AUDIO_RETENTION_SECONDS=0  # Ephemeral, no storage
- PARENT_CONSENT_REQUIRED=true
- SESSION_LOG_LEVEL=error  # No info/debug logging of child audio
"@

# --- 3. STT SERVICE (Deepgram Streaming) ---
Write-Host "`n[3] STT SERVICE (services/stt.py)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
@"
Implement Deepgram Nova-3 streaming:
- WebSocket connection: wss://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen
- Encoding: linear16 (ESP32 I2S output)
- Sample rate: 16000 (ESP32 INMP441)
- Channels: 1 (mono)
- Smart formatting: true
- Interim results: true (for progressive UX)
- Endpointing: 300ms (detect silence)
- Language: en-US (default), it-IT (heritage mode)

Return: Generator of transcript chunks with timestamps.
No audio storage. Stream directly to LLM.
"@

# --- 4. LLM SERVICE (Groq Prompt Router) ---
Write-Host "`n[4] LLM SERVICE (services/llm.py)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
@"
Implement Groq API with dynamic prompt routing:
- Base model: llama-3.3-70b-versatile
- Fallback: llama-3.1-8b-instant (for simple modes)
- Temperature: 0.7 (creative), 0.3 (STEM/homework)
- Max tokens: 500 (Cartesia limit), 150 for short responses

PROMPT ROUTER:
10 characters × 13 modes = 130 configurations.
Load from JSON files in /prompts/ directory:
/prompts/characters/{character_id}.json
/prompts/modes/{mode_id}.json

Merge at runtime:
system_prompt = character.personality + mode.instructions + age_context

Example Orsetto + Lullaby:
"You are Orsetto, a warm little bear who loves adventures. 
Now you are in Lullaby mode. Speak softly, slowly, and gently. 
Help Marco (3 years old) fall asleep. [whisper] Close your eyes..."

Cache prompts in Redis (1 hour TTL) to avoid reload.
"@

# --- 5. TTS SERVICE (Cartesia SSML Prosody) ---
Write-Host "`n[5] TTS SERVICE (services/tts.py)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
@"
Implement Cartesia Sonic 3 with SSML prosody control:
- API: https://api.cart      Output stopped                                          But your right go ahead and route it to

# CASA COMPANION VOICE AGENT BACKEND
# Cloud-agnostic, ESP32-S3 compatible, toddler-grade latency

## STACK (NO AZURE)
- STT: Deepgram Nova-3 (streaming, 300ms p95)
- LLM: Groq LPU Llama 3.3 70B (500+ tps, $0.59/1M input)
- TTS: Cartesia Sonic 3 (40ms, SSML prosody, 500char limit)
- Session: Redis (Fly.io or self-hosted)
- Transport: WebSocket (WiFi), BLE (device sync)

## CONSTRAINTS
- ESP32-S3: 240MHz, 512KB RAM, 8MB flash
- Audio: I2S DMA, 16kHz, 16-bit mono
- Battery: 800mAh, 4-6 hours active
- Latency budget: <800ms end-to-end (toddler tolerance)
- COPPA: Ephemeral audio, no storage, parental consent

## DELIVERABLES NEEDED

### 1. SESSION MANAGER (Redis)
- Shared conversation state across wearable + Tap Board + TV
- Device handoff: BLE proximity triggers session migration
- Auto-cleanup: 30s idle timeout, COPPA compliance
- Parent kill switch: immediate session termination

### 2. PROMPT ROUTER (130 configs)
- 10 characters × 13 modes = dynamic system prompt loading
- Character voice ID maps to Cartesia voice profile
- Mode maps to SSML prosody tags (lullaby=whisper, stem=excited)
- Groq prompt caching for hot configs

### 3. AUDIO PIPELINE (WebSocket)
- ESP32 → server: Opus-encoded audio stream (low bitrate)
- Server → Deepgram: real-time streaming STT
- Turn detection: server-side VAD, not ESP32
- Response streaming: chunk TTS for 500char Cartesia limit

### 4. DEVICE FIRMWARE (ESP32-S3)
- WakeNet local wake word ("Hey Orsetto", "Hey Drago", etc.)
- WiFi audio streaming to server WebSocket
- BLE GAP/GATT for Tap Board sync + NFC state
- I2S speaker playback with jitter buffer
- Power management: deep sleep between turns

### 5. COPPA COMPLIANCE LAYER
- Parental consent verification before first session
- Audio ephemerality: process → respond → delete
- No persistent logs, no analytics on voice data
- Real-time dashboard: active session only, no history

-- Parent accounts (verifiable consent)
CREATE TABLE parents (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  email text UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  consent_verified boolean DEFAULT false,
  consent_method text CHECK (consent_method IN ('credit_card','video_call','signed_form')),
  consent_verified_at timestamptz,
  created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()
);

-- Devices (wearable, Tap Board, TV dongle)
CREATE TABLE devices (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  parent_id uuid REFERENCES parents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  device_type text CHECK (device_type IN ('wearable','tap_board','tv_dongle')),
  character_id text, -- active character
  mode_id text, -- active mode
  last_seen_at timestamptz,
  battery_level int CHECK (battery_level BETWEEN 0 AND 100),
  is_online boolean DEFAULT false
);

-- Active sessions (ephemeral, auto-cleanup)
CREATE TABLE sessions (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  parent_id uuid REFERENCES parents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  device_id uuid REFERENCES devices(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  character_id text NOT NULL,
  mode_id text NOT NULL,
  started_at timestamptz DEFAULT now(),
  last_activity_at timestamptz DEFAULT now(),
  is_active boolean DEFAULT true,

  CONSTRAINT ephemeral CHECK (is_active = true OR last_activity_at > now() - interval '30 seconds')
);

-- Character configs (130 combos, seeded data)

  character_id text NOT NULL,
  mode_id text NOT NULL,

);

**Key changes for your stack:**
- **Supabase** replaces Redis for session state + device presence
- **Vercel Edge Functions** replace custom server (but WebSocket is tricky on Vercel — may need custom server or SSE fallback)
- **Supabase Realtime** handles device sync instead of custom WebSocket
- **COPPA consent** integrated into Supabase Auth flow

**Vercel WebSocket reality:** Vercel Functions don't support native WebSocket (10-30s timeout). Options:
1. **SSE (Server-Sent Events)** from server → ESP32, HTTP POST from ESP32 → server
2. **Custom server** on Fly.io/Hetzner for WebSocket, Vercel for dashboard
3. **Supabase Realtime** as the WebSocket layer (max 500-2000 connections)

The prompt includes all three options — `ok-computer` will pick the best for your scale.

**Key fix:** Fly.io handles the WebSocket, Vercel handles the dashboard. No more Vercel timeout headaches.

**Fly.io specifics:**
- `auto_stop_machines = 'off'` — voice server stays always-on
- `min_machines_running = 1` — at least one instance up
- Shared CPU + 512MB RAM is enough for the audio pipeline (STT/LLM/TTS are all API calls, not local compute)

$prompt = @"
BUILD: Casa Companion Voice Agent Backend
Stack: Fly.io + Vercel + Supabase + Groq + Cartesia + Deepgram
NO Azure. COPPA-compliant. Toddler-grade latency.

FLY.IO (Voice Server - always-on WebSocket):
- FastAPI app, native WebSocket /ws/voice/{device_id}
- Accept Opus audio from ESP32-S3
- Stream to Deepgram Nova-3 STT
- Route to Groq Llama 3.3 70B with 130 character+mode prompts
- Chunk to Cartesia Sonic 3 TTS (500char limit, SSML prosody)
- Return audio chunks to ESP32 via WebSocket
- Ephemeral: no audio storage, process→respond→delete
- Supabase: device registry, session tracking, parent auth
- fly.toml: auto_stop_machines = 'off', min_machines_running = 1

VERCEL (Next.js Dashboard):
- /dashboard: parent live session view (SSE from Fly.io)
- /api/consent: COPPA verification (Stripe $1 hold)
- /api/devices: device registration
- /api/kill: proxy to Fly.io kill switch
- No WebSocket on Vercel. SSE only.

SUPABASE (Database/Auth):
- parents: email, consent_verified, consent_method
- devices: device_type, character_id, mode_id, battery, fly_machine_id
- sessions: ephemeral, 30s timeout, no audio/transcript storage
- character_modes: 130 seeded configs (character+mode, prompt, voice_id, ssml_template)
- medallions: nfc_tag_id, character_id, mode_id

ESP32-S3 FIRMWARE:
- WakeNet local wake word (Hey Orsetto, Hey Drago, etc.)
- I2S DMA audio → Opus encode → WebSocket to Fly.io
- BLE GAP/GATT for Tap Board sync
- PN532 NFC reader → HTTP POST medallion tap
- Deep sleep between turns, battery reporting
- Jitter buffer → I2S speaker playback

COPPA:
- Verifiable parental consent before first use
- No audio persistence, no transcript storage
- 24hr log retention on Fly.io only
- Parent delete cascades, 30-day hard delete

ERROR HANDLING:
- Deepgram timeout >500ms: retry once, fallback audio
- Groq 429: exponential backoff, max 3 retries
- Cartesia 500char: chunk at sentence boundary
- ESP32 disconnect: 30s session timeout
- Parent kill: immediate broadcast termination
- Battery <10%: sleep command

DELIVER: Complete working code. No placeholders. No TODOs.
- FastAPI voice server (main.py, session_manager, prompt_router, audio_pipeline, coppa_layer)
- Dockerfile + fly.toml
- Next.js app (dashboard, API routes, middleware)
- ESP32 C skeleton (audio_task, wifi_task, ble_task, nfc_task, wake_word, power_mgmt)
- Supabase schema + seed SQL
- README with env vars and deploy commands
"@

**User:** ok i need a simplke prompt to audit the casa companion repos lcoally and then on our new git hub then main codebasew is on vercel and i do not believe we have hooked up the vercel side to the git hub so we have deploymentrs all over the lace with 5 branches  pertosnal ones like liam , peter jimmy, jenny and the main one

**Assistant:** Got it. Let me build you a single, clean prompt that audits everything locally, on GitHub, and flags the Vercel disconnect + branch chaos. No preamble, just the prompt. Memory updated       Here is your single Kimi Code prompt. Copy-paste into PowerShell and run:powershell   Copy # === KIMI CODE PROMPT: CASA COMPANION AUDIT & CONSOLIDATION ===
# Run this in Kimi Code terminal (PowerShell)

# --- CONFIG ---
$localRepo = "C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\casa-companion"
$githubRepo = "https://github.com/simplebalance89-ai/casa-companion"
$vercelProject = "app-wheat-seven-35.vercel.app"  # update if different
$branches = @("main", "liam", "peter", "jimmy", "jenny")

# --- STEP 1: LOCAL AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== LOCAL REPO AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Set-Location $localRepo
git status
git branch -a
git log --oneline -5
git remote -v

# Check for uncommitted changes
$uncommitted = git status --short
if ($uncommitted) { Write-Host "⚠️ UNCOMMITTED CHANGES FOUND:" -ForegroundColor Red; $uncommitted } 
else { Write-Host "✅ Working tree clean" -ForegroundColor Green }

# Check if local is ahead/behind remote
$branchInfo = git branch -vv
Write-Host "`n--- Branch sync status ---"
$branchInfo

# --- STEP 2: GITHUB REMOTE AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== GITHUB REMOTE AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
# Fetch all remote branches without changing local state
git fetch --all
$remoteBranches = git branch -r
Write-Host "Remote branches:"
$remoteBranches

# Check latest commit on each branch
foreach ($branch in $branches) {
    $remoteBranch = "origin/$branch"
    if ($remoteBranches -match $remoteBranch) {
        $lastCommit = git log -1 --format="%h | %s | %cr" $remoteBranch
        Write-Host "$branch`: $lastCommit"
    } else {
        Write-Host "$branch`: ❌ NOT ON REMOTE" -ForegroundColor Red
    }
}

# --- STEP 3: VERCEL DEPLOYMENT AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== VERCEL DEPLOYMENT AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "Checking Vercel project: $vercelProject"
Write-Host "Manual check needed: Go to https://vercel.com/dashboard and verify:"
Write-Host "  1. Is $vercelProject connected to $githubRepo ?"
Write-Host "  2. Which branch is set as Production Branch?"
Write-Host "  3. Are there Preview Deployments for each branch?"

# Check if Vercel CLI is installed and logged in
$vercelCli = Get-Command vercel -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
if ($vercelCli) {
    Write-Host "`nVercel CLI found. Running project list..."
    vercel projects list 2>$null | Select-String "casa-companion"
} else {
    Write-Host "Vercel CLI not found. Install: npm i -g vercel" -ForegroundColor Yellow
}

# --- STEP 4: BRANCH COMPARISON (THE CHAOS MAP) ---
Write-Host "`n=== BRANCH COMPARISON (Chaos Map) ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
$mainHead = git rev-parse origin/main

foreach ($branch in $branches | Where-Object { $_ -ne "main" }) {
    $remoteBranch = "origin/$branch"
    if ($remoteBranches -match $remoteBranch) {
        $diffCount = git rev-list --count "$mainHead..$remoteBranch"
        $aheadCount = git rev-list --count "$remoteBranch..$mainHead"
        $mergeBase = git merge-base $mainHead $remoteBranch
        
        Write-Host "`n$branch vs main:"
        Write-Host "  Ahead of main: $diffCount commits"
        Write-Host "  Behind main: $aheadCount commits"
        Write-Host "  Diverged from: $(git log -1 --format='%h | %cr' $mergeBase)"
        
        # Show files that differ
        $diffFiles = git diff --name-only "$mainHead...$remoteBranch"
        if ($diffFiles) {
            Write-Host "  Files changed:"
            $diffFiles | ForEach-Object { Write-Host "    - $_" }
        }
    }
}

# --- STEP 5: CONSOLIDATION PLAN OUTPUT ---
Write-Host "`n=== CONSOLIDATION PLAN ===" -ForegroundColor Green
Write-Host @"

RECOMMENDED FIX ORDER:
1. PICK THE SOURCE OF TRUTH
   - Compare: local main vs GitHub main vs Vercel deployed code
   - Decide which is the ACTUAL production codebase

2. FIX VERCEL-GITHUB CONNECTION
   - If Vercel project is NOT linked to GitHub:
     a) Go to Vercel Dashboard > Project Settings > Git
     b) Connect to: $githubRepo
     c) Set Production Branch to: main
   - If already connected, verify auto-deploy is ON

3. MERGE OR DELETE PERSONAL BRANCHES
   - For each branch (Liam, Peter, Jimmy, Jenny):
     a) Check if changes are valuable → PR to main
     b) If stale/duplicate → Delete branch
     c) If WIP → Rename to feature/xxx

4. CLEAN LOCAL REPO
   - git checkout main
   - git pull origin main
   - Delete merged local branches: git branch -d liam peter jimmy jenny

5. VERIFY DEPLOYMENT PIPELINE
   - Push to main → Vercel auto-deploys
   - PR to main → Vercel Preview Deploy
   - No more direct deployments to Vercel without Git

"@

# --- STEP 6: SAVE REPORT ---
$reportPath = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\casa-companion-audit-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm').txt"
@"
CASA COMPANION AUDIT REPORT
Generated: $(Get-Date)
Local Repo: $localRepo
GitHub: $githubRepo
Vercel: $vercelProject

BRANCH STATUS:
$($remoteBranches | Out-String)

UNCOMMITTED CHANGES:
$($uncommitted | Out-String)

RECOMMENDATION: See consolidation plan above.
"@ | Out-File $reportPath

Write-Host "`n📄 Report saved to: $reportPath" -ForegroundColor Green What this does in one shot:Local audit — checks your working tree, branches, remotes, uncommitted changesGitHub audit — fetches all branches, shows last commit on each (Liam, Peter, Jimmy, Jenny, main)Vercel disconnect check — tells you exactly what to verify in the Vercel dashboard + checks if CLI is availableBranch chaos map — counts how far each personal branch is ahead/behind main, lists files that differConsolidation plan — 5-step fix order with the Vercel-Github hookup as step 2Saves a report to your Desktop with timestampAfter you run it, paste the output here and I'll tell you exactly which branch to keep, which to merge, and how to wire Vercel to GitHub properly.                                               yes the push to giut hub with the latest has not happend in the cli https://github.com/GCEstack/gce-ai-casa-companion

Copy-paste into Kimi Code PowerShell and run:powershell   Copy # === KIMI CODE PROMPT: CASA COMPANION — PUSH + AUDIT + CONSOLIDATE ===
# Run this in Kimi Code terminal (PowerShell)

# --- CONFIG ---
$localRepo = "C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\casa-companion"
$githubRepo = "https://github.com/GCEstack/gce-ai-casa-companion"
$vercelProject = "app-wheat-seven-35.vercel.app"  # update if different
$branches = @("main", "liam", "peter", "jimmy", "jenny")

# --- STEP 0: PUSH LATEST LOCAL TO GITHUB ---
Write-Host "`n=== STEP 0: PUSH LOCAL LATEST TO GITHUB ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Set-Location $localRepo

# Verify remote points to correct repo
$currentRemote = git remote get-url origin 2>$null
if ($currentRemote -ne $githubRepo) {
    Write-Host "⚠️ Remote mismatch. Current: $currentRemote" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Fixing remote to point to $githubRepo"
    git remote set-url origin $githubRepo
}

# Check if we have changes to push
$localStatus = git status --short
$localCommits = git log --oneline origin/main..HEAD 2>$null

if ($localStatus -or $localCommits) {
    Write-Host "Local changes/commits found. Pushing to GitHub..." -ForegroundColor Green
    
    # Stage everything if uncommitted
    if ($localStatus) {
        git add -A
        git commit -m "sync: local updates before audit $(Get-Date -Format 'yyyy-MM-dd')"
    }
    
    # Push all branches
    git push origin main
    foreach ($branch in $branches | Where-Object { $_ -ne "main" }) {
        $branchExists = git branch --list $branch
        if ($branchExists) {
            git push origin $branch --force-with-lease 2>$null
            if ($?) { Write-Host "Pushed $branch" } else { Write-Host "Skipped $branch (may not exist locally)" }
        }
    }
} else {
    Write-Host "Local is already in sync with GitHub main" -ForegroundColor Green
}

# --- STEP 1: LOCAL AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== LOCAL REPO AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
git status
git branch -a
git log --oneline -5
git remote -v

$uncommitted = git status --short
if ($uncommitted) { Write-Host "⚠️ UNCOMMITTED CHANGES:" -ForegroundColor Red; $uncommitted } 
else { Write-Host "✅ Working tree clean" -ForegroundColor Green }

$branchInfo = git branch -vv
Write-Host "`n--- Branch sync status ---"
$branchInfo

# --- STEP 2: GITHUB REMOTE AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== GITHUB REMOTE AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
git fetch --all
$remoteBranches = git branch -r
Write-Host "Remote branches:"
$remoteBranches

foreach ($branch in $branches) {
    $remoteBranch = "origin/$branch"
    if ($remoteBranches -match $remoteBranch) {
        $lastCommit = git log -1 --format="%h | %s | %cr" $remoteBranch
        Write-Host "$branch`: $lastCommit"
    } else {
        Write-Host "$branch`: ❌ NOT ON REMOTE" -ForegroundColor Red
    }
}

# --- STEP 3: VERCEL DEPLOYMENT AUDIT ---
Write-Host "`n=== VERCEL DEPLOYMENT AUDIT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "Project: $vercelProject"
Write-Host "GitHub: $githubRepo"
Write-Host "`nManual check needed at https://vercel.com/dashboard:"
Write-Host "  1. Is $vercelProject connected to $githubRepo ?"
Write-Host "  2. Production Branch = main?"
Write-Host "  3. Preview Deployments for branches enabled?"

$vercelCli = Get-Command vercel -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
if ($vercelCli) {
    Write-Host "`nVercel CLI found. Project list:"
    vercel projects list 2>$null | Select-String "casa-companion"
} else {
    Write-Host "Vercel CLI not found. Install: npm i -g vercel" -ForegroundColor Yellow
}

# --- STEP 4: BRANCH CHAOS MAP ---
Write-Host "`n=== BRANCH CHAOS MAP ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
$mainHead = git rev-parse origin/main

foreach ($branch in $branches | Where-Object { $_ -ne "main" }) {
    $remoteBranch = "origin/$branch"
    if ($remoteBranches -match $remoteBranch) {
        $diffCount = git rev-list --count "$mainHead..$remoteBranch"
        $aheadCount = git rev-list --count "$remoteBranch..$mainHead"
        $mergeBase = git merge-base $mainHead $remoteBranch
        
        Write-Host "`n$branch vs main:"
        Write-Host "  Ahead of main: $diffCount commits"
        Write-Host "  Behind main: $aheadCount commits"
        Write-Host "  Diverged from: $(git log -1 --format='%h | %cr' $mergeBase)"
        
        $diffFiles = git diff --name-only "$mainHead...$remoteBranch"
        if ($diffFiles) {
            Write-Host "  Files changed:"
            $diffFiles | ForEach-Object { Write-Host "    - $_" }
        }
    } else {
        Write-Host "$branch`: ❌ No remote branch found" -ForegroundColor Red
    }
}

# --- STEP 5: CONSOLIDATION PLAN ---
Write-Host "`n=== CONSOLIDATION PLAN ===" -ForegroundColor Green
Write-Host @"

FIX ORDER:
1. ✅ PUSH LOCAL LATEST (done above)
2. FIX VERCEL-GITHUB CONNECTION
   - Vercel Dashboard > Project Settings > Git
   - Connect to: $githubRepo
   - Production Branch: main
   - Enable Preview Deployments for all branches
3. MERGE OR DELETE PERSONAL BRANCHES
   - Liam, Peter, Jimmy, Jenny:
     a) Valuable changes → PR to main
     b) Stale/duplicate → Delete
     c) WIP → Rename to feature/xxx
4. CLEAN LOCAL
   - git checkout main && git pull origin main
   - git branch -d liam peter jimmy jenny (if merged)
5. VERIFY PIPELINE
   - Push main → Vercel auto-deploys
   - PRs → Preview Deployments
   - No direct Vercel deployments without Git

"@

# --- STEP 6: SAVE REPORT ---

@"
CASA COMPANION AUDIT REPORT

Vercel: $vercelProject

This is a common problem with mobile browsers:Autoplay policy: Mobile browsers (iOS Safari, Chrome Android) block autoplay of audio unless the user has interacted with the page first. Since the audio is triggered programmatically after the API call returns, it might be blocked.Audio context: On mobile, the Audio context needs to be created/resumed after a user gesture (tap, click).The fix: Need to unlock audio context on first user interaction (Connect button click), then use the same context for playback.The fix is to create/resume an AudioContext on the Connect button click, then use that context for all subsequent audio playback. The screen sits recessed inside the box, behind the lid. The lid plastic protects the screen. Kid pokes the lid, not the screen.Or: Add a layer of clear polycarbonate or acrylic over the screen inside the box. Double protection.The recessed approach:plain   Copy ┌─────────────────────┐
│  Gratury box lid    │  ← plastic protects screen
│  [cut window here]  │
│  ┌─────────────┐    │
│  │  acrylic    │    │  ← extra layer
│  │  sheet      │    │
│  │  ┌───────┐  │    │
│  │  │ LCD   │  │    │  ← screen recessed
│  │  │screen │  │    │
│  │  └───────┘  │    │
│  └─────────────┘    │
└─────────────────────┘ The screen is protected by:The box lid plastic (first barrier)Optional acrylic sheet (second barrier)Recessed position (can't be hit directly)This is how real kid products are built. **Assistant:** I see them — NTAG215 NFC stickers, 25mm, 20-pack, adhesive back. $6.90, already purchased May 16.NTAG215 is perfect. Here's why:Table  Copy   SpecWhat it means NTAG215504 bytes memory — enough for a URL or UUID25mm1 inch — good size for kid to tap, not too smallAdhesive backStick to book pages, cards, box surface20-packMultiple tags for different characters/modesThe ACR122U reader you ordered reads NTAG215. No compatibility issues.What we put on the tags:Option A: Full URL — https://casa-frontend.fly.dev/character/tartaruga/story-time (takes more space, needs internet)Option B: Short UUID — tartaruga-story (504 bytes is plenty, Pi resolves it locally or via backend)For our build, we use Option B. The tag stores a simple ID like TAG001. The Pi reads it, looks up what TAG001 means in a local file, and shows the right Tartaruga content.This means the tags are dumb and reusable. We can reprogram them anytime to point to different characters or modes. No need to buy new tags.You're good. The NTAG215 stickers + ACR122U reader = perfect combo.Anything else you need to check before the deliveries start hitting? ## Git Worktree Setup Guide
- Account: account1

**User:** README.md md 1.35 kb    Okay. Good. Can you explain something to me? Again, I work on basically here. I'll give you the file. Multiple portals, whatever. Search Git worktrees best practices 2026 multiple repos monorepo Vercel deployment GitHub worktree production staging workflow GitHub multiple environments deployment branches vs worktrees  22 results          GitHub Gist Git Worktrees: From Zero to Hero 3 weeks agoAlright, let me break this down for you. You understand the basics—GitHub → Vercel deploy, branches, PRs. But you're missing the local workspace architecture that makes multi-agent, multi-project work actually sane. Here's what you're probably not doing and why it matters.The Core Problem You're FacingRight now you're probably doing this:One local clone per repoSwitching branches inside that single folderStashing work when you need to hotfix somethingMaybe multiple full clones eating disk spaceAgents (Kimi, Claude, etc.) all stepping on each other in the same working directoryWorktrees fix this. They let you have multiple working directories (full checkouts) tied to a single Git repository. Same .git object store, different branches checked out simultaneously in different folders. No stashing, no "oh shit I was in the middle of something," no clone bloat. The Proper Setup: Bare Clone + WorktreesThis is the pattern the pnpm team and serious agent orchestrators use. Step 1: Bare clone as your hubpowershell   Copy # Create a hub directory
mkdir C:\Claude\Projects\casa-companion-hub
cd C:\Claude\Projects\casa-companion-hub

# Bare clone (no working files, just Git data)
git clone --bare https://github.com/GCEstack/gce-ai-casa-companion.git casa-companion.git
cd casa-companion.git Step 2: Create worktrees as siblingspowershell   Copy # Main branch always checked out
git worktree add ../main main

# Feature work for you
git worktree add -b feature/hero-video ../feature-hero main

# Agent isolation—Kimi Code works here
git worktree add -b agent/kimi-audit ../agent-kimi main

# Hotfix branch ready to go
git worktree add -b hotfix/api-timeout ../hotfix-api main Resulting structure:plain   Copy casa-companion-hub/
├── casa-companion.git/     # Bare repo (shared Git objects)
├── main/                   # Your stable main checkout
├── feature-hero/           # Your feature work
├── agent-kimi/             # Kimi Code agent workspace
└── hotfix-api/             # Emergency fixes Each folder is a full working directory with its own branch, its own files, its own node_modules if needed. But they all share the same underlying Git history—so disk usage is minimal. Why This Matters for Your Agent WorkflowYou run Kimi Code, Claude Code, Cursor, whatever. Without worktrees, they're all fighting over the same files or you're cloning the repo 4 times. With worktrees:Kimi Code gets ../agent-kimi — it can edit, build, break things without touching your main workClaude Code gets ../agent-claude — parallel work on a different featureYou stay in ../main or ../feature-hero — your context is preservedCompare implementations side-by-side in your IDE: code ../feature-hero ../agent-kimi The pnpm team literally uses this for "AI-assisted development" — they spin up worktrees per agent task. The Pre-Warmed Pool Pattern (Critical for Speed)Here's where you probably get fucked: creating a worktree is instant, but npm install / pnpm install is NOT. If every agent spin-up costs you a 10-minute dependency install, you won't use worktrees for small tasks. The fix: Keep a pool of "warm" worktrees with dependencies already installed.powershell   Copy # Create 3 warm slots
git worktree add ../slot-a main
git worktree add ../slot-b main
git worktree add ../slot-c main

# Install deps in each (one-time cost)
cd ../slot-a && pnpm install
cd ../slot-b && pnpm install
cd ../slot-c && pnpm install When an agent needs to work:Grab a warm slotgit pull origin main (fast)git checkout -b agent/task-123 (instant)Work, commit, pushgit checkout main && git pull to reset the slot for next timeIf lockfile changed, reinstall. Otherwise, near-instant agent spin-up. What You're Missing: Environment ManagementYou mentioned Vercel deploy from GitHub. That's fine. But the branch-per-environment model (dev branch, staging branch, prod branch) is an anti-pattern for GitOps. Don't do this:main → productionstaging → staging branchdev → dev branchMerging between them for "promotion"Why it breaks: A merge from staging to prod doesn't just bring your code—it brings every config tweak, replica count change, environment variable someone tweaked in staging. You end up with "configuration drift" and merge conflicts that have nothing to do with your actual feature. Do this instead:One branch: main (trunk-based development)Vercel Preview Deployments for every PR (automatic)Production deploys from tagged releases on main, not branch mergesEnvironment-specific config lives in Vercel dashboard / Azure Key Vault / .env.production, NOT in Git branchesYour Git history stays clean. Your deploys are deterministic. ## Rules

- Create the hub as a NEW sibling folder: `[repo-name]-hub`

## Per-Repo Steps (repeat for each)

For each repo, do exactly this:

1. **Check current repo status**
   - `cd` into the existing repo folder

2. **Create hub folder** (if not already exists)
   - Path: `C:\Claude\Projects\[repo]-hub` for Projects
   - Path: `C:\Claude\Personal\[repo]-hub` for Personal
   - Do NOT proceed if the existing repo folder is missing or not a git repo

3. **Bare clone**
   - `git clone --bare <origin-url> [repo].git`
   - OR if no remote: `git clone --bare <local-path> [repo].git`

4. **Configure bare repo**
   - `cd [repo].git`
   - `git config remote.origin.fetch "+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*"`
   - `git fetch origin` (or `git fetch` if local-only)

5. **Create worktrees**

   - `git worktree add -b agent/claude-review ../agent-claude main`
   - `git worktree add -b feature/active-work ../feature-active main`

6. **Verify**
   - `git worktree list`

7. **Confirm with me**

## Sacred Code Rules (NEVER TOUCH)
These files/patterns are OFF LIMITS for any agent modification:
- Any file containing `DATABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`
- Any file with `AZURE_OPENAI_KEY`, `AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`
- Any `.env` file (`.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.production`, `.env.example`)
- Database schema files (`schema.sql`, `migrations/`, `prisma/schema.prisma` if exists)
- API route files in `web-next/pages/api/` or `web-revamp/src/pages/api/` that handle auth or data persistence
- Core voice/realtime WebRTC handshake logic (if exists in `services/ai/realtime.ts`)
- Any file with `JWT_SECRET`, `ADMIN_PASSPHRASE`, `INTERNAL_API_KEY`

Agent can READ these files for context. Agent must NEVER EDIT, DELETE, or REFACTOR them.

## Review Checklist

### 1. Architecture Overview
- List all frontend apps (web-next, web-revamp, web-mobile, tv-companion, voice-agent)
- List all backend services/agents
- Map the data flow: user → frontend → backend → database → AI provider
- Identify which AI providers are used (OpenAI, Groq, Deepgram, Azure, etc.)

### 2. Frontend Review (per app)
- Tech stack (Next.js version, React version, Tailwind?, TypeScript?)
- Build status — does it compile?
- Deployment target (Vercel? Azure? other?)
- Environment variables exposed client-side (VITE_*, NEXT_PUBLIC_*)
- Critical: any API keys leaked to browser?

### 3. Backend Review
- API framework (FastAPI? Express? Next.js API routes?)
- Database (Supabase? Postgres? other?)
- AI provider integration pattern
- Voice/realtime implementation
- Deployment target

### 4. Security Audit
- Client-side API keys (check all `.env.example` files)
- Hardcoded credentials in source
- `.env.production` or `.env` files not in `.gitignore`
- Supabase keys exposed
- OpenAI/Deepgram keys in browser bundle

### 5. Deployment & Config
- Vercel connection status
- GitHub push status (is local ahead of remote?)
- Azure container app status (if applicable)
- Build scripts and CI/CD

### 6. Issues & Opportunities
- What's broken?
- What's missing?
- What's duplicated?
- What should be consolidated?
- Recommended next actions (prioritized)

- Scan ALL files: .py, .js, .ts, .json, .yaml, .yml, .toml, .sh, .ps1, .md

## SEARCH PATTERNS — FIND ALL OF THESE

### 1. MCP (Model Context Protocol) Indicators
- "mcp" in filenames or content
- "model_context_protocol" 
- "mcp_server", "mcp_client", "mcp-tool"
- "@modelcontextprotocol" (npm packages)
- "stdio" transport references
- "sse" transport references

### 2. CLI Tool Indicators  
- "argparse", "click", "typer", "fire" (Python CLI libs)
- "commander", "yargs", "minimist", "oclif" (Node CLI libs)
- "#!/usr/bin" or "#!/bin/bash" shebangs
- "bin": entries in package.json
- "console_scripts" in setup.py / pyproject.toml
- "scripts": entries in package.json
- Any file with "cli" in name: *cli*.py, *cli*.js, cli/*.js
- PowerShell functions with [CmdletBinding()] or param blocks
- Any .exe, .bat, .cmd references in code
- "subprocess.run", "os.system", "child_process" calls

### 3. Terminal / Shell Integrations
- .ps1, .sh, .bash, .zsh files
- PowerShell modules (.psm1, .psd1)
- Terminal emulators or PTY references
- "xterm", "node-pty", "pty.js" 
- Shell escape sequences or ANSI codes

### 4. API / SDK Wrappers (CLI-like interfaces)
- Any wrapper around external APIs that exposes commands
- "api_client", "sdk", "wrapper" with command methods
- Azure CLI references ("az ")
- AWS CLI references ("aws ")
- gcloud, kubectl, docker, npm, npx, pip, poetry, uv

   Summary of runway repo audit

   Project: Runway — Next.js 14 music discovery dashboard with Supabase, Spotify, and Tidal OAuth.

   🔴 Critical (2)

   1. Hardcoded Supabase service-role + anon keys in 8 files (scripts/*.mjs, agents/runway_client.py). The service-role key bypasses RLS and exposes the
   user_tokens table containing Spotify/Tidal tokens.
   2. Command injection in /api/feed/scrape via execSync with interpolated user input.

   🟠 High (3)

   3. Middleware doesn't verify JWT signature — only checks token shape, so auth is bypassable.
   4. Custom /api/auth/login reimplements Supabase auth and sets cookies manually.
   5. Missing authorization checks on playlist delete/sync/edit — any user can mutate any playlist.

   🟡 Medium (5)

   6. agent_runs UPDATE RLS policy allows any authenticated user to update any run.
   7. tracks INSERT policy allows spoofing discovered_by.
   8. .env.local.example tracked in Git.
   9. Python venv/ inside the Node repo.
   10. Verbose logging of partial credentials in Tidal OAuth routes.

   🟢 Low / Info (4)

   11. README is significantly out of date.
   12. Missing input validation on several routes.
   13. Overly broad Tidal image remote pattern.
   14. Hardcoded production URL fallback.

   Top 3 actions you should take right now

   1. Rotate the Supabase service-role key and move all keys to env vars.
   2. Fix the command injection in app/api/feed/scrape/route.ts (use execFile).
   3. Rewrite middleware.ts to use @supabase/ssr and actually validate the session.

   Want me to start fixing any of these issues?

## PART 1: FRONTEND FORENSICS
Analyze the Next.js frontend layer in voice-agent/dashboard/ or equivalent:

- Component hierarchy: map every page, layout, and shared component
- State management: how is voice state, character selection, and session data handled?
- Asset pipeline: how do images load? Lazy loading? Video preloading? WebGL/Three.js usage?
- Voice UI: microphone component, transcript display, character response rendering
- Character cards: how are they rendered? Static or dynamic? Image-to-video transition logic
- Mobile: responsive breakpoints, touch handling, audio autoplay policies
- Performance: bundle analysis, Core Web Vitals, Lighthouse score estimate
- Accessibility: ARIA labels, keyboard navigation, focus management, screen reader support

Identify specific files and line numbers for:
- Memory leaks (event listeners not cleaned up)
- Race conditions (async voice state updates)
- Re-render storms (React.memo missing, context misuse)
- Audio context suspension issues (browser autoplay blocking)

## PART 2: BACKEND FORENSICS
Analyze the FastAPI backend in voice-agent/backend/ or equivalent:

- Endpoint inventory: list every route, method, payload shape, response schema
- Voice pipeline: request flow from mic → transcription → LLM → TTS → playback
- Concurrency: how many simultaneous voice sessions? WebSocket vs HTTP polling?
- Database: schema, migrations, connection pooling, query patterns
- Caching: Redis? In-memory? TTL strategies?
- Error handling: try/catch coverage, fallback behaviors, retry logic
- Rate limiting: per-user, per-endpoint, global?
- Logging: structured? PII scrubbing? Alerting hooks?

Identify specific files and line numbers for:
- Unhandled exceptions (missing try/catch)
- N+1 queries (inefficient DB patterns)
- Blocking I/O in async routes
- Missing input validation (pydantic gaps)
- Secret leakage (hardcoded keys, debug endpoints)

## PART 3: SECURITY AUDIT (Apply Runway lessons learned)
Based on the RUNWAY_SECURITY_AUDIT.md findings, audit Casa Companion for:
- [ ] Hardcoded API keys (OpenAI, Azure, Groq, Whisper, ElevenLabs)

- [ ] File upload handling: path traversal, mime type validation
- [ ] Rate limiting: missing or bypassable?
- [ ] Environment variable exposure: .env in git, debug logs leaking secrets

## PART 4: MCP MIGRATION — TIDAL & SPOTIFY
Design a Kimi-native MCP (Model Context Protocol) integration:

### Tidal MCP
- OAuth flow: user connects Tidal → tokens stored securely → Kimi can query
- Capabilities:
  - Search tracks, albums, artists
  - Get user playlists
  - Create/modify playlists
  - Get playback state/recommendations
- Kimi prompts for:
  - 'Find me songs like [character vibe] — e.g., Pietro's morning coffee playlist'
  - 'Create a playlist for [character] based on their personality'
  - 'What's trending in [genre] this week?'

### Spotify MCP
- OAuth flow: parallel to Tidal, shared token storage
- Capabilities:
  - Search, user playlists, recommendations, audio features
  - Cross-reference with Tidal for availability
- Kimi prompts for:
  - 'Build a playlist for Cassita beach vibes'
  - 'Find underground tracks matching [character] energy'
  - 'Sync this Tidal playlist to Spotify'

### Unified Music Agent
- Single interface that queries BOTH Tidal and Spotify
- Fallback logic: if track unavailable on one, check the other
- Playlist generation: Kimi creates based on character personality + user context
- Output: generate .mcp JSON configs and Kimi prompt templates

## Kimi Work Scheduler Integration

**User:** Is there a way I could schedule tasks from Kimi app on my phone or the Kimi desktop to when we create things power show will pick up those task in the work session and run them

**Assistant:** Search Kimi AI schedule tasks PowerShell automation Kimi Code PowerShell integration task scheduling Kimi AI agent scheduled execution workflow  22 results    Yes, absolutely. The bridge between Kimi (app/desktop) and PowerShell execution is Kimi Work — the desktop local agent — which has a built-in Cron scheduler and can execute shell/Python tasks on your machine. Here's how to wire it up:The ArchitectureKimi Work (Desktop) → Cron Scheduler → PowerShell Script → Your Work SessionKimi Work runs locally, mounts your folders, and has a Cron engine that can trigger LLM agent calls, Python executions, or shell commands at scheduled intervals. It also supports "Keep Computer Awake" for overnight tasks. Two Ways to Set This UpOption 1: Kimi Work Native Cron (Recommended)Kimi Work has a built-in scheduler that supports:LLM Agent Calls — Trigger a Kimi agent to run a specific taskPython/Shell executions — Run PowerShell scripts directlyConditional triggers — Daily, hourly, or custom intervalsHow to configure:Open Kimi Work desktop appCreate a Skill (reusable instruction package) that defines what PowerShell should doUse the Cron scheduler to set when it runs (e.g., daily at 8 AM, or every 4 hours)Toggle "Keep Computer Awake" if you need overnight executionThe agent executes quietly in the background and writes outputs to your local foldersExample Skill prompt for PowerShell execution:plain   Copy Run PowerShell script at C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\scripts\daily-sync.ps1
Capture output to C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\logs\sync-$(Get-Date -Format yyyyMMdd).log
If errors occur, summarize them and save to error-log.txt Option 2: Windows Task Scheduler + Kimi Work BridgeIf you want more granular Windows-native control, use Task Scheduler to kick off PowerShell, but have Kimi Work generate/maintain the scripts:powershell   Copy # === KIMI WORK → POWERSHELL SCHEDULER ===
# Save this as: C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\scripts\kimi-task-runner.ps1

param(
    [string]$TaskName = "default",
    [string]$WorkingDir = "C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\",
    [string]$LogDir = "C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\logs\"
)

$timestamp = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd_HH-mm"
$logFile = Join-Path $LogDir "kimi-task-$TaskName-$timestamp.log"

# Create log directory if missing
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $LogDir | Out-Null

# Write header
"=== KIMI TASK: $TaskName | $(Get-Date) ===" | Out-File $logFile -Append

# Your task logic here - this is what Kimi Work would generate/update
switch ($TaskName) {
    "repo-scan" {
        # Example: scan your repos
        "Running repo scan..." | Out-File $logFile -Append
        # Add your actual commands
    }
    "casa-companion-deploy" {
        "Deploying Casa Companion updates..." | Out-File $logFile -Append
    }
    "epo-crowdsource-sync" {
        "Syncing EPO Crowdsource..." | Out-File $logFile -Append
    }
    default {
        "No task handler for: $TaskName" | Out-File $logFile -Append
    }
}

"=== COMPLETED: $(Get-Date) ===" | Out-File $logFile -Append Then register it with Windows Task Scheduler:powershell   Copy # === REGISTER SCHEDULED TASK ===
$action = New-ScheduledTaskAction `
    -Execute "powershell.exe" `
    -Argument "-NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File `"C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\scripts\kimi-task-runner.ps1`" -TaskName `"repo-scan`""

$trigger = New-ScheduledTaskTrigger -Daily -At 8am

$principal = New-ScheduledTaskPrincipal `
    -UserId "$env:USERDOMAIN\$env:USERNAME" `
    -LogonType Interactive `
    -RunLevel Highest

$settings = New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet `
    -AllowStartIfOnBatteries `
    -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries `
    -StartWhenAvailable

Register-ScheduledTask `
    -TaskName "KimiWork-RepoScan" `
    -Action $action `
    -Trigger $trigger `
    -Principal $principal `
    -Settings $settings `
    -Description "Kimi Work scheduled task - runs PowerShell script for repo scanning" The Workflow LoopYou create/schedule tasks in Kimi (app or desktop) — describe what you want doneKimi Work generates the PowerShell script and saves it to your scripts folderKimi Work Cron or Windows Task Scheduler picks it up at the scheduled timePowerShell runs it — writes logs, modifies files, deploys to Azure, etc.Next Kimi session — you or the AI reads the logs and continues from where it left offKimi Code IntegrationSince you use Kimi Code in PowerShell already, you can have the scheduled task invoke Kimi Code directly:powershell   Copy # In your scheduled script, call Kimi Code for autonomous execution
kimi --session "nightly-tasks" --yolo "Review C:\Users\Dekan AI Brother\runway\ for any new images, generate videos using the hero pipeline, and save to the videos folder. Log all actions." This uses Kimi Code's session persistence (--session) and auto-approval (--yolo) for fully hands-off execution. Bottom LineKimi Work desktop is the missing link. It has the scheduler, it runs locally, it can execute PowerShell, and it maintains context across sessions. You schedule tasks in natural language, Kimi Work translates that to PowerShell, and your Windows machine runs them on autopilot.Want me to build out the full PowerShell scaffold for your specific projects (Casa Companion, EPO, edge-crew) with the task definitions and scheduler registration?                                       Reference             Yes, please

## Mic transcript glitch
- Account: account1

**User:** Everything’s working pretty good except now on the phone of the only one I texted that when I use the microphone doesn’t torque back it writes a transcript

**Assistant:** Robust Microphone Component State Management Mobile Handling Transcript Control    The user is reporting a bug with a mobile interface where the microphone button doesn't "torque back" (return to its original state) after use, and instead writes a transcript. This is related to the Casa Companion project based on recent conversation history.Let me look at the context:Casa Companion is a frontend project with voice commandsThe user mentioned adding voice commands to charactersThe issue is specifically on mobile (phone) where the microphone button doesn't return to its original state after recording, and instead writes a transcriptThis sounds like a speech recognition / Web Speech API issue where:The microphone button doesn't visually reset after recording endsThe transcript is being written to the page instead of being processed as a commandI need to provide a fix for the microphone UI state management and transcript handling on mobile. Since this is a specific bug fix request and the user wants working code, I should provide the relevant JavaScript/CSS fix.Given the user's style (prefers direct script delivery, no long explanations), I should provide the fix immediately.The issue is likely:onresult handler is appending text directlyonend or onerror handler isn't resetting the microphone button stateMobile Safari/Chrome has different behavior for SpeechRecognition eventsI'll provide a robust microphone component fix that:Properly manages the UI state (recording/stopped)Handles the transcript correctly without writing it to the pageHandles mobile-specific quirks (SpeechRecognition can be flaky on mobile)Ensures the button returns to original stateSince I don't have the exact current code, I'll provide a complete, drop-in replacement for the microphone/voice command component that handles state properly.   Here’s the fix for the microphone state bug on mobile. Drop this into your voice command handler:JavaScript   Copy // === MOBILE MICROPHONE STATE FIX ===
class VoiceCommandController {
  constructor() {
    this.recognition = null;
    this.isListening = false;
    this.micButton = document.getElementById('mic-button'); // adjust selector
    this.transcriptBuffer = '';
    this.restartDelay = 300;
    this.init();
  }

  init() {
    if (!('webkitSpeechRecognition' in window) && !('SpeechRecognition' in window)) {
      console.warn('Speech API not supported');
      return;
    }

    const SpeechRecognition = window.SpeechRecognition || window.webkitSpeechRecognition;
    this.recognition = new SpeechRecognition();
    this.recognition.continuous = false; // mobile: single utterance per tap
    this.recognition.interimResults = false;
    this.recognition.lang = 'en-US';
    this.recognition.maxAlternatives = 1;

    this.bindEvents();
    this.bindButton();
  }

  bindEvents() {
    // Fires when speech is recognized
    this.recognition.onresult = (event) => {
      const transcript = event.results[0][0].transcript.trim().toLowerCase();
      this.transcriptBuffer = transcript;
      console.log('Voice input:', transcript);
      this.processCommand(transcript);
    };

    // CRITICAL: Reset UI on ANY end condition
    this.recognition.onend = () => {
      this.isListening = false;
      this.resetMicUI();
      // Auto-restart only if explicitly toggled (optional)
    };

    // Handle errors without breaking UI
    this.recognition.onerror = (event) => {
      console.error('Speech error:', event.error);
      this.isListening = false;
      this.resetMicUI();
      
      // Mobile-specific: no-speech often fires on short taps
      if (event.error === 'no-speech' || event.error === 'aborted') {
        this.showToast('No speech detected. Try again.');
      }
    };

    // Mobile Safari sometimes fires onstart late — force UI sync
    this.recognition.onstart = () => {
      this.isListening = true;
      this.setMicActive();
    };
  }

  bindButton() {
    if (!this.micButton) return;
    
    // Use pointer events for better mobile response
    this.micButton.addEventListener('pointerdown', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault(); // prevent zoom/scroll
      this.toggleMic();
    });

    // Prevent double-fire on some mobile browsers
    this.micButton.addEventListener('click', (e) => e.preventDefault());
  }

  toggleMic() {
    if (this.isListening) {
      this.stopListening();
    } else {
      this.startListening();
    }
  }

  startListening() {
    try {
      this.transcriptBuffer = '';
      this.recognition.start();
      // Optimistic UI update (in case onstart delays)
      this.setMicActive();
    } catch (err) {
      // Already started — reset and retry
      if (err.message?.includes('already started')) {
        this.recognition.stop();
        setTimeout(() => this.startListening(), this.restartDelay);
      }
    }
  }

  stopListening() {
    try {
      this.recognition.stop();
    } catch (e) {
      // Ignore stop errors
    }
    this.isListening = false;
    this.resetMicUI();
  }

  setMicActive() {
    if (!this.micButton) return;
    this.micButton.classList.add('mic-active', 'recording');
    this.micButton.classList.remove('mic-idle');
    this.micButton.setAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');
    // Haptic feedback if available
    if (navigator.vibrate) navigator.vibrate(50);
  }

  resetMicUI() {
    if (!this.micButton) return;
    this.micButton.classList.remove('mic-active', 'recording');
    this.micButton.classList.add('mic-idle');
    this.micButton.setAttribute('aria-pressed', 'false');
  }

  processCommand(transcript) {
    // === YOUR COMMAND ROUTING HERE ===
    // Don't write to page — route to your character handlers
    const event = new CustomEvent('voicecommand', { 
      detail: { transcript, timestamp: Date.now() } 
    });
    document.dispatchEvent(event);
  }

  showToast(msg) {
    // Optional: flash message without breaking layout
    const toast = document.createElement('div');
    toast.className = 'voice-toast';
    toast.textContent = msg;
    document.body.appendChild(toast);
    setTimeout(() => toast.remove(), 2000);
  }
}

// === CSS (add to your stylesheet) ===
/*
.mic-button {
  transition: transform 0.2s ease, background-color 0.2s ease;
  transform: scale(1);
  -webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent; /* remove mobile tap flash */
}

.mic-button.recording {
  transform: scale(1.1);
  background-color: #ff4444; /* red recording state */
  animation: pulse 1.5s infinite;
}

@keyframes pulse {
  0%, 100% { opacity: 1; }
  50% { opacity: 0.7; }
}

.voice-toast {
  position: fixed;
  bottom: 100px;
  left: 50%;
  transform: translateX(-50%);
  background: rgba(0,0,0,0.8);
  color: white;
  padding: 12px 24px;
  border-radius: 24px;
  font-size: 14px;
  z-index: 9999;
  pointer-events: none;
}
*/

// Init
const voice = new VoiceCommandController(); Key fixes for your bug:pointerdown + preventDefault() — stops the mobile browser from writing the transcript to the page or triggering zoom/scrollrecognition.continuous = false — mobile handles single utterances better; continuous mode often hangs the UIonend always resets UI — guarantees the button returns to idle even if speech is empty or errorsonerror catches no-speech — the most common mobile failure; prevents stuck "recording" stateNo transcript written to DOM — routes through CustomEvent instead so nothing leaks onto the pageIf your button selector is different, change document.getElementById('mic-button') to match your markup. ## PowerShell Repo Scan
- Account: account1
- Created: 2026-07-03T02:42:28Z

**User:** can u create a kimi code powershell prompt to search my whole computer for all repos local and any folders that are named the same or simliar and give a full audit on how many there are if they have reme and how many can et archived vs combined an dupdated as of 1 month

**Assistant:** I can create a comprehensive Kimi Code PowerShell prompt for you. This will audit all local repositories, detect duplicates/similar names, check remotes, and categorize them by action (archive, combine, update).Here's the complete prompt:KIMI CODE POWERSHELL — FULL REPO AUDIT & DEDUPLICATION ENGINECopy-paste this into Kimi Code:powershell   Copy # ============================================
# KIMI CODE: FULL REPO AUDIT & DEDUPLICATION ENGINE
# ============================================
# Run: kimi-code powershell -f repo-audit.ps1
# Or paste into Kimi Code terminal

# CONFIGURATION
$AuditDate = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd_HH-mm"
$OutputPath = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\RepoAudit_$AuditDate"
$OneMonthAgo = (Get-Date).AddMonths(-1)

# Create output directory
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $OutputPath -Force | Out-Null

# ============================================
# PHASE 1: DISCOVER ALL REPOS
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n🔍 PHASE 1: Scanning entire system for Git repositories..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

$RepoLocations = @(
    "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\Documents",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\Projects",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\source",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\repos",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\github",
    "$env:USERPROFILE\git",
    "C:\Projects",
    "C:\Repos",
    "C:\Dev",
    "C:\Code"
)

$AllRepos = @()
foreach ($location in $RepoLocations) {
    if (Test-Path $location) {
        $repos = Get-ChildItem -Path $location -Recurse -Depth 3 -Filter ".git" -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
        foreach ($repo in $repos) {
            $repoPath = $repo.Parent.FullName
            $repoName = $repo.Parent.Name
            
            # Get last modified date
            $lastModified = (Get-Item $repoPath).LastWriteTime
            
            # Check for remote
            $remote = $null
            try {
                $remote = git -C $repoPath remote -v 2>$null | Select-Object -First 1
                if ($remote) {
                    $remote = ($remote -split '\s+')[1]
                }
            } catch {}
            
            # Check if dirty/uncommitted
            $status = "clean"
            try {
                $gitStatus = git -C $repoPath status --porcelain 2>$null
                if ($gitStatus) { $status = "dirty" }
            } catch {}
            
            # Get branch count
            $branches = 0
            try {
                $branches = (git -C $repoPath branch -a 2>$null).Count
            } catch {}
            
            $AllRepos += [PSCustomObject]@{
                Name = $repoName
                FullPath = $repoPath
                ParentFolder = $repo.Parent.Parent.FullName
                LastModified = $lastModified
                IsRecent = $lastModified -gt $OneMonthAgo
                HasRemote = [bool]$remote
                RemoteUrl = $remote
                GitStatus = $status
                BranchCount = $branches
                SizeMB = [math]::Round((Get-ChildItem $repoPath -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Measure-Object -Property Length -Sum).Sum / 1MB, 2)
            }
        }
    }
}

# Also scan entire C: drive for .git folders (deep scan)
Write-Host "🌐 Running deep scan of C: drive for any missed repos..." -ForegroundColor Yellow
$DeepRepos = Get-ChildItem -Path C:\ -Recurse -Depth 4 -Filter ".git" -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | 
    Where-Object { $_.FullName -notlike "*node_modules*" -and $_.FullName -notlike "*/.*" } |
    ForEach-Object {
        $repoPath = $_.Parent.FullName
        $repoName = $_.Parent.Name
        $lastModified = (Get-Item $repoPath).LastWriteTime
        
        $remote = $null
        try {
            $remote = git -C $repoPath remote -v 2>$null | Select-Object -First 1
            if ($remote) { $remote = ($remote -split '\s+')[1] }
        } catch {}
        
        [PSCustomObject]@{
            Name = $repoName
            FullPath = $repoPath
            ParentFolder = $_.Parent.Parent.FullName
            LastModified = $lastModified
            IsRecent = $lastModified -gt $OneMonthAgo
            HasRemote = [bool]$remote
            RemoteUrl = $remote
            GitStatus = "unknown"
            BranchCount = 0
            SizeMB = [math]::Round((Get-ChildItem $repoPath -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Measure-Object -Property Length -Sum).Sum / 1MB, 2)
        }
    }

$AllRepos = ($AllRepos + $DeepRepos) | Sort-Object FullPath -Unique

# Export full inventory
$AllRepos | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\1-FULL-INVENTORY.csv" -NoTypeInformation
Write-Host "✅ Found $($AllRepos.Count) total repositories" -ForegroundColor Green

# ============================================
# PHASE 2: SIMILARITY & DUPLICATION DETECTION
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n🔍 PHASE 2: Analyzing similar names and duplicates..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

# Group by exact name
$ExactDuplicates = $AllRepos | Group-Object Name | Where-Object { $_.Count -gt 1 }

# Fuzzy similarity detection (Levenshtein-style simple matching)
function Get-SimilarityScore {
    param([string]$s1, [string]$s2)
    $s1 = $s1.ToLower().Replace("-","").Replace("_","").Replace(" ","")
    $s2 = $s2.ToLower().Replace("-","").Replace("_","").Replace(" ","")
    
    if ($s1 -eq $s2) { return 100 }
    if ($s1.Contains($s2) -or $s2.Contains($s1)) { return 85 }
    
    # Simple character overlap
    $set1 = $s1.ToCharArray() | Sort-Object -Unique
    $set2 = $s2.ToCharArray() | Sort-Object -Unique
    $intersection = ($set1 | Where-Object { $set2 -contains $_ }).Count
    $union = ($set1 + $set2 | Sort-Object -Unique).Count
    return [math]::Round(($intersection / $union) * 100)
}

$SimilarGroups = @()
$processed = @()

for ($i = 0; $i -lt $AllRepos.Count; $i++) {
    if ($processed -contains $i) { continue }
    
    $group = @($AllRepos[$i])
    $processed += $i
    
    for ($j = $i + 1; $j -lt $AllRepos.Count; $j++) {
        if ($processed -contains $j) { continue }
        
        $score = Get-SimilarityScore $AllRepos[$i].Name $AllRepos[$j].Name
        if ($score -ge 70) {
            $group += $AllRepos[$j]
            $processed += $j
        }
    }
    
    if ($group.Count -gt 1) {
        $SimilarGroups += [PSCustomObject]@{
            GroupID = $SimilarGroups.Count + 1
            PrimaryName = $group[0].Name
            MatchCount = $group.Count
            Similarity = "High"
            Paths = ($group.FullPath -join "`n")
            TotalSizeMB = ($group | Measure-Object -Property SizeMB -Sum).Sum
            HasRemotes = ($group | Where-Object { $_.HasRemote }).Count
            AllRemotes = ($group | Where-Object { $_.RemoteUrl } | Select-Object -ExpandProperty RemoteUrl -Unique) -join "`n"
        }
    }
}

# Export duplicates
$ExactDuplicates | Select-Object Name, Count, @{N='Paths';E={($_.Group.FullPath -join "`n")}} | 
    Export-Csv "$OutputPath\2-EXACT-DUPLICATES.csv" -NoTypeInformation

$SimilarGroups | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\3-SIMILAR-NAMES.csv" -NoTypeInformation
Write-Host "✅ Found $($ExactDuplicates.Count) exact duplicates, $($SimilarGroups.Count) similar groups" -ForegroundColor Green

# ============================================
# PHASE 3: ACTION CATEGORIZATION
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n🔍 PHASE 3: Categorizing actions..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

$ArchiveCandidates = @()
$CombineCandidates = @()
$UpdateCandidates = @()
$KeepAsIs = @()

foreach ($repo in $AllRepos) {
    # Archive: No remote, old, clean, single branch
    if (-not $repo.HasRemote -and -not $repo.IsRecent -and $repo.GitStatus -eq "clean" -and $repo.BranchCount -le 1 -and $repo.SizeMB -lt 50) {
        $ArchiveCandidates += $repo
    }
    # Combine: Part of a duplicate/similar group
    elseif (($ExactDuplicates | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $repo.Name }) -or 
            ($SimilarGroups | Where-Object { $_.Paths -contains $repo.FullPath })) {
        $CombineCandidates += $repo
    }
    # Update: Has remote but not recent, or dirty
    elseif ($repo.HasRemote -and (-not $repo.IsRecent -or $repo.GitStatus -eq "dirty")) {
        $UpdateCandidates += $repo
    }
    else {
        $KeepAsIs += $repo
    }
}

# Export categorized lists
$ArchiveCandidates | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\4-ARCHIVE-CANDIDATES.csv" -NoTypeInformation
$CombineCandidates | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\5-COMBINE-CANDIDATES.csv" -NoTypeInformation
$UpdateCandidates | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\6-UPDATE-CANDIDATES.csv" -NoTypeInformation
$KeepAsIs | Export-Csv "$OutputPath\7-KEEP-AS-IS.csv" -NoTypeInformation

# ============================================
# PHASE 4: GENERATE EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n📊 PHASE 4: Generating executive summary..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

$TotalSize = ($AllRepos | Measure-Object -Property SizeMB -Sum).Sum
$RemoteCount = ($AllRepos | Where-Object { $_.HasRemote }).Count
$RecentCount = ($AllRepos | Where-Object { $_.IsRecent }).Count
$DirtyCount = ($AllRepos | Where-Object { $_.GitStatus -eq "dirty" }).Count

$Summary = @"
# REPOSITORY AUDIT REPORT
**Generated:** $(Get-Date)
**Auditor:** Kimi Code PowerShell Engine
**Cutoff Date:** $OneMonthAgo

## 📊 HIGH-LEVEL METRICS
| Metric | Count |
|--------|-------|
| **Total Repositories** | $($AllRepos.Count) |
| **Total Size** | $([math]::Round($TotalSize, 2)) MB |
| **With Remotes** | $RemoteCount |
| **Updated (Last 30 Days)** | $RecentCount |
| **Uncommitted Changes** | $DirtyCount |
| **Exact Duplicates** | $($ExactDuplicates.Count) |
| **Similar Names** | $($SimilarGroups.Count) groups |

## 🎯 ACTION BREAKDOWN
| Action | Count | Est. Space Saved |
|--------|-------|------------------|
| **🗑️ ARCHIVE** | $($ArchiveCandidates.Count) | $([math]::Round(($ArchiveCandidates | Measure-Object -Property SizeMB -Sum).Sum, 2)) MB |
| **🔗 COMBINE** | $($CombineCandidates.Count) | $([math]::Round(($CombineCandidates | Measure-Object -Property SizeMB -Sum).Sum / 2, 2)) MB |
| **⬆️ UPDATE** | $($UpdateCandidates.Count) | - |
| **✅ KEEP** | $($KeepAsIs.Count) | - |

## 🔗 REMOTE REPOSITORIES
$($AllRepos | Where-Object { $_.HasRemote } | ForEach-Object { "- [$($_.Name)]($($_.RemoteUrl)) - $($_.FullPath)" } | Out-String)

## 🗑️ ARCHIVE CANDIDATES (Safe to Zip/Delete)
$($ArchiveCandidates | ForEach-Object { "- **$($_.Name)** | $($_.FullPath) | Last: $($_.LastModified.ToString('yyyy-MM-dd')) | $($_.SizeMB)MB" } | Out-String)

## 🔗 COMBINE CANDIDATES (Merge These)
$($SimilarGroups | ForEach-Object { 
    "### Group $($_.GroupID): $($_.PrimaryName) ($($_.MatchCount) matches)`n$($_.Paths -split "`n" | ForEach-Object { "- $_" } | Out-String)`n**Suggested:** Keep one with remote, archive others`n" 
} | Out-String)

## ⬆️ UPDATE CANDIDATES (Git Pull/Push Needed)
$($UpdateCandidates | ForEach-Object { "- **$($_.Name)** | $($_.GitStatus) | $($_.FullPath)" } | Out-String)

## 📁 OUTPUT FILES
All detailed CSVs saved to: $OutputPath

---
**Next Steps:**
1. Review `4-ARCHIVE-CANDIDATES.csv` → Zip and delete
2. Review `5-COMBINE-CANDIDATES.csv` → Merge repos
3. Run `git pull` on `6-UPDATE-CANDIDATES.csv`
"@

$Summary | Out-File "$OutputPath\0-EXECUTIVE-SUMMARY.md" -Encoding utf8

# ============================================
# PHASE 5: GENERATE ACTION SCRIPTS
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n⚡ PHASE 5: Generating action scripts..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

# Archive script
$ArchiveScript = @"
# AUTO-GENERATED ARCHIVE SCRIPT
# Run with: .\archive-repos.ps1
# BACKUP BEFORE RUNNING!

`$ArchiveDate = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd"
`$ArchiveDir = "`$env:USERPROFILE\ArchivedRepos_`$ArchiveDate"
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path `$ArchiveDir -Force

"@ + ($ArchiveCandidates | ForEach-Object { 
    "`$source = '$($_.FullPath -replace "'", "''")'`n`$name = '$($_.Name -replace "'", "''")'`nCompress-Archive -Path `$source -DestinationPath `"`$ArchiveDir\`$name.zip`" -Force`nWrite-Host `"Archived: `$name`"`n# Remove-Item `$source -Recurse -Force # UNCOMMENT TO DELETE`n" 
} | Out-String)

$ArchiveScript | Out-File "$OutputPath\ACTION-archive-repos.ps1" -Encoding utf8

# Update script
$UpdateScript = @"
# AUTO-GENERATED UPDATE SCRIPT
# Run with: .\update-repos.ps1

"@ + ($UpdateCandidates | Where-Object { $_.HasRemote } | ForEach-Object { 
    "`$path = '$($_.FullPath -replace "'", "''")'`nWrite-Host `"Updating: $($_.Name)`" -ForegroundColor Cyan`ngit -C `$path fetch --all`ngit -C `$path pull --all`n" 
} | Out-String)

$UpdateScript | Out-File "$OutputPath\ACTION-update-repos.ps1" -Encoding utf8

# ============================================
# FINAL OUTPUT
# ============================================
Write-Host "`n✅ AUDIT COMPLETE!" -ForegroundColor Green
Write-Host "📁 All reports saved to: $OutputPath" -ForegroundColor Yellow
Write-Host "`n📊 SUMMARY:" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "   Total Repos: $($AllRepos.Count)" -ForegroundColor White
Write-Host "   🗑️ Archive: $($ArchiveCandidates.Count)" -ForegroundColor Red
Write-Host "   🔗 Combine: $($CombineCandidates.Count)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
Write-Host "   ⬆️ Update: $($UpdateCandidates.Count)" -ForegroundColor Blue
Write-Host "   ✅ Keep: $($KeepAsIs.Count)" -ForegroundColor Green

# Open folder
Invoke-Item $OutputPath WHAT THIS DOES:Table  Copy   PhaseAction 1Scans common dev folders + deep C: drive scan for .git folders2Detects exact duplicates and fuzzy similar names (70%+ match)3Categorizes: Archive (no remote, old, clean), Combine (duplicates), Update (has remote, dirty/old), Keep4Generates EXECUTIVE-SUMMARY.md with full markdown report5Auto-generates ready-to-run PowerShell scripts for archiving and updatingOUTPUT FILES:0-EXECUTIVE-SUMMARY.md — Full markdown report1-FULL-INVENTORY.csv — Every repo found2-EXACT-DUPLICATES.csv — Same name, different paths3-SIMILAR-NAMES.csv — Fuzzy matches (e.g., casa-companion vs casa_companion)4-ARCHIVE-CANDIDATES.csv — Safe to zip/delete5-COMBINE-CANDIDATES.csv — Merge these6-UPDATE-CANDIDATES.csv — Run git pull7-KEEP-AS-IS.csv — Active projectsACTION-archive-repos.ps1 — One-click archive scriptACTION-update-repos.ps1 — One-click update scriptTo run in Kimi Code:Save as repo-audit.ps1Run: kimi-code powershell -f repo-audit.ps1Or paste directly into Kimi Code terminalWant me to add GitHub API integration to check if remotes are ahead/behind, or add a "merge repos" script for the combine candidates?                                                       Ask away. Pics work too.                       K2.6 Instant
